<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For a finite <i>p</i>-group <i>G</i> and a subgroup <i>H</i>, we prove that the optimal chromatic fixed-point loss equals the shortest length of a subnormal chain from <i>H</i> to <i>G</i> with cyclic quotients. The equality holds at every prime and every nonnegative height, resolving positively the equality proposed by Kuhn and Lloyd.

- **claim-main-equality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every prime $p$, finite $p$-group $G$, subgroup $H\le G$, and integer $n\ge0$, $$\boxed{r_n(G,H)=\ell(G,H).}$$
  - Explanation: For every finite p-group and every subgroup, the optimal chromatic fixed-point loss equals the shortest length of a subnormal chain from the subgroup to the group with cyclic quotients, at every prime and every nonnegative height.
- **claim-cyclic-upper-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cyclic-upper)): For every finite $p$-group $G$, subgroup $H$, and $n\ge0$, $r_n(G,H)\le\ell(G,H)$.
  - Explanation: The loss is at most the cyclic subnormal length, obtained by applying the cyclic chromatic Smith theorem step by step along a chain.
- **claim-uniform-finite-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-local)): Let $\mathcal C_m^G$ be the category of genuine $G$-spectra localized by the inflated sphere $L_m^fS$. It is rigidly compactly generated. Its compact tensor-triangular spectrum is the finite subspace $$\{(H,i):H\le G\text{ up to conjugacy},\ 0\le i\le m\}$$ of the equivariant spectrum, with $(H,i)$ representing $\mathcal P_i^G(H)$. The image of a finite genuine spectrum is compact and dualizable. An object $Z\in\mathcal C_m^G$ is zero if and only if $$T(i)\wedge\Phi^H Z=0 \qquad(H\le G,\ 0\le i\le m).$$ For the image of a finite object, its support is tested equally by $T(i)$ and $K(i)$ at these heights. For every compact object of $\mathcal C_m^G$, membership in the support at $(H,i)$ is equivalent to nonvanishing of $K(i)\wedge\Phi^H(-)$.
  - Explanation: The finite localization of genuine G-spectra is rigidly compactly generated; its compact spectrum is a finite set of subgroup-height points, and support and vanishing are tested by telescopes or Morava K-theories at the retained heights.
- **claim-occurrence-fixed-points** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-occurrence-fixed-points)): For every finite group $L$ and ordinary commutative ring spectrum $R$, there is an equivalence of rational commutative ring spectra $$\label{eq:general-full-mark-comparison} e_L^L(R^{BL})_{\mathbb Q}\simeq\bigl(U_L(R)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}.$$ In particular, $$D_L\ne0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \bigl(U_L(\mathbf 1)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: The full-mark summand of rationalized cochains is equivalent to the rationalized geometric fixed points of the cofree spectrum, so occurrence of a group is the same as nonvanishing of those fixed points.
- **claim-occurrence-sections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-occurrence-sections)): Every occurring finite group is a $p$-group. The class of occurring groups is closed under subgroups and quotients. If $A$ is any section of an occurring group, its minimum number of generators satisfies $d(A)\le l$.
  - Explanation: Occurring groups are p-groups, closed under subgroups and quotients, and every section of one is generated by at most the fixed height number of elements.
- **claim-pro-character-tower** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-pro-characters)): Suppose $P$ is a finite group with $(U_P(\mathbf 1))_{\mathbb Q}\ne0$. There is a tower of occurring finite $p$-groups $$\cdots\twoheadrightarrow L_2\twoheadrightarrow L_1 \twoheadrightarrow L_0=P, \qquad \Gamma=\varprojlim_i L_i,$$ with the following properties.* 1. *Every finite section $A$ of $\Gamma$ has $d(A)\le l$. Every closed subgroup of $\Gamma$ is topologically generated by at most $l$ elements; in particular $\Gamma$ is finitely generated and $\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow P$.* 2. *For each finite collection of finite groupoids $Y$, at every sufficiently advanced stage $L=L_i$ inflation is an equivalence $$\label{eq:mapping-groupoid-stabilization} \mathop{\mathrm{Fun}}(BL,Y)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal X_\Gamma(Y).$$ At those stages the evaluation maps give an isomorphism of $D_L$-algebras in $\mathcal C_{\mathbb Q}$, $$\label{eq:character-splitting} D_L\otimes A_Y\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \prod_{[\alpha]\in\pi_0\mathcal X_\Gamma(Y)}D_L.$$ These isomorphisms are natural for pullback along maps of the selected groupoids. In particular, a common stage can be chosen for a finite diagram of finite groupoids, including both its bases and its total groupoids.
  - Explanation: Maximizing homomorphism classes along a tower of occurring p-groups produces a finitely generated pro-p group with bounded sections, whose cochains split into evaluation coordinates at all sufficiently advanced stages.
- **claim-cover-evaluation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-evaluation)): Let $f:Y\to Z$ be a finite covering of finite groupoids. Choose a common stage $L$ as in [thm:pro-characters](#thm:pro-characters), and a label $\alpha:BL\to Z$. If the pullback of $f$ is the finite $L$-set $S$, then the evaluation of $f_!(1)\in\pi_0 A_Z$ at $\alpha$ is $\|S^L\|\,1_{D_L}$.
  - Explanation: The transfer of the unit along a finite covering evaluates at a label as the number of fixed points of the pulled-back finite set times the unit.
- **claim-integration-weights** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-relative-weights)): In the coordinates <a href="#eq:groupoid-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:groupoid-coordinates">[eq:groupoid-coordinates]</a>, the $D$-linear extension of integration $\epsilon_Y:A_Y\to\mathbf 1$ is the map $$\label{eq:absolute-weights} (x_\alpha)_{[\alpha]}\longmapsto \sum_{[\alpha]}\frac{x_\alpha}{a_\alpha}.$$ For a map $f:Y\to Z$, its pushforward has coordinate formula $$\label{eq:relative-weights} \bigl(f_!(x)\bigr)_\beta =\sum_{[\alpha]:[f\alpha]=[\beta]} \frac{a_\beta}{a_\alpha}\,x_\alpha.$$ Consequently $\chi_\beta(f_!1)$ is the rational scalar $$\label{eq:relative-fiber-mass} \left\| \mathcal X_\Gamma(Y) \mathop{\times}^{h}_{\mathcal X_\Gamma(Z)}\{\beta\} \right\|_{\mathrm h}$$ times the unit of $D$.
  - Explanation: In evaluation coordinates, integration is a sum weighted by inverse automorphism counts, pushforward has an explicit weighted coordinate formula, and the character value of a pushforward unit is a homotopy cardinality of a fibre.
- **claim-scalar-integrality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scalar-integrality)): Let $F$ be finite, let $z\in\pi_0A_F$, and suppose that an evaluation at an advanced stage satisfies $\chi_\alpha(z)=r\cdot1_D$ for $r\in\mathbb Q$. Then $r\in\mathbb Z_{(p)}$. If the restriction of $z$ to the underlying point of $BF$ is zero in $\pi_0\mathbf 1$, then $r\in p\mathbb Z_{(p)}$.
  - Explanation: A rational evaluation scalar of an integral cochain is p-locally integral, and lies in the ideal generated by p when the point restriction vanishes; the proof uses invertibility of one plus p times an element.
- **claim-hom-integrality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hom-integrality)): For every finite group $F$, $$\label{eq:hom-integrality} \frac{\|\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma,F)\|}{\|F\|}\in\mathbb Z_{(p)}.$$
  - Explanation: For every finite group, the number of continuous homomorphisms from the pro-p group divided by the group order is p-locally integral.
- **claim-subgroup-congruence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-subgroup-congruence)): For every open subgroup $U\le_o\Gamma$, every finite continuous $U$-set $X$, and every $h\geq0$, $$\label{eq:subgroup-congruence} \sum_{\substack{V\le_o U\\{}[U:V]=p^h}} \bigl(\|X^V\|-\|X\|\bigr) \in p^{h+1}\mathbb Z_{(p)}.$$ The sum is over all open subgroups of the indicated index.
  - Explanation: The sum over open subgroups of a fixed p-power index of the deviation of fixed-point counts from the size of the set lies in the corresponding power of p times the p-local integers.
- **claim-procyclic-series** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-procyclic)): Let $\Gamma$ be a finitely generated pro-$p$ group, and let $l\geq0$. Suppose that $d(F)\leq l$ for every finite section $F$ of $\Gamma$, that $$\label{eq:profinite-hom} \frac{\|\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma,F)\|}{\|F\|}\in\mathbb Z_{(p)} \qquad\text{for every finite group }F,$$ and that, for every $U\leq_o\Gamma$, every finite continuous $U$-set $X$, and every $h\geq0$, $$\label{eq:profinite-congruences} \sum_{\substack{V\leq_o U\\{}[U:V]=p^h}} \bigl(\|X^V\|-\|X\|\bigr) \in p^{h+1}\mathbb Z_{(p)}.$$ Then $\Gamma$ has a series of closed subgroups $$1=K_t\trianglelefteq K_{t-1}\trianglelefteq\cdots \trianglelefteq K_0=\Gamma, \qquad t\leq l,$$ whose nontrivial factors are isomorphic to $\mathbb Z_p$. In particular, every finite quotient $P$ of $\Gamma$ satisfies $\ell(P,1)\leq l$.
  - Explanation: A finitely generated pro-p group with bounded sections, integral homomorphism counts and the subgroup congruences has a closed subnormal series of bounded length with trivial or Z_p factors, so every finite quotient has bounded cyclic length.
- **claim-profinite-elementary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profinite-elementary)): Let $E$ be a pro-$p$ group.* 1. *Every finite section of $E$ is a section of a finite quotient of $E$. If all finite sections of $E$ have generator number at most $l$, then every closed subgroup of $E$ is topologically generated by at most $l$ elements.* 2. *A procyclic pro-$p$ group is either trivial, finite cyclic, or isomorphic to $\mathbb Z_p$. Every continuous epimorphism $K\twoheadrightarrow \mathbb Z_p$ splits.* 3. *A finitely generated pro-$p$ group with infinite abelianization has a continuous epimorphism onto $\mathbb Z_p$.
  - Explanation: Elementary facts on pro-p groups: bounded generator number of finite sections gives topological generation of closed subgroups, procyclic groups are classified and surjections onto Z_p split, and infinite abelianization yields a surjection onto Z_p.
- **claim-shrinking-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-shrinking-action)): Let $K$ be a pro-$p$ group and let $d,l\geq0$. Suppose $K\times\mathbb Z_p^d$ satisfies the subgroup congruences and all its finite sections have generator number at most $l$. If $K\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb Z_p$ has kernel $N$, then $N\times\mathbb Z_p^{d+1}$ has both properties.
  - Explanation: Splitting off a Z_p quotient and replacing the semidirect product by a direct product keeps both the subgroup congruences and the section generator bound.
- **claim-lattice-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-limit)): For $d\geq1$, let $a_j(d)$ be the number of open subgroups of index $p^j$ in $\mathbb Z_p^d$. Then $$\label{eq:lattice-generating-function} \begin{aligned} \sum_{j\geq0}a_j(d)t^j &=\prod_{u=0}^{d-1}(1-p^u t)^{-1},\\ \lim_{j\to\infty}a_j(d) &=c_d:=\prod_{u=1}^{d-1}(1-p^u)^{-1}\ne0 \quad\text{in }\mathbb Z_p. \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The generating function for open subgroups of p-power index in Z_p^d is an explicit product, and the counts converge p-adically to a nonzero limit.
- **claim-kernel-abelianization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-abelianization)): Let $K$ be a nontrivial finitely generated pro-$p$ group and $d\geq1$. If $K\times\mathbb Z_p^d$ satisfies the subgroup congruences, then $K$ has infinite abelianization.
  - Explanation: A nontrivial finitely generated pro-p kernel whose product with Z_p^d satisfies the subgroup congruences must have infinite abelianization, by comparing two ways of counting subgroups that project onto it.
- **claim-rational-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-rational-gluing)): For every finite $p$-group $P$ and every $l\geq0$, $$\bigl(U_P(L_{T(l)}S)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}\ne0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \ell(P,1)\leq l.$$
  - Explanation: Rational nonvanishing of the cofree geometric fixed points of the telescopic sphere forces the cyclic subnormal length of the p-group to be at most the height.
- **claim-product-series** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-product-series)): Let $P$ be a finite $p$-group, and let $k,l\geq0$. If $$\ell\bigl(P\times(C_{p^r})^k,1\bigr)\leq l \qquad\text{for every }r\geq1,$$ then $k+\ell(P,1)\leq l$.
  - Explanation: If products with ever larger cyclic factors keep the cyclic length bounded, an inverse limit argument shows the free rank can be subtracted from the bound.
- **claim-ring-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-ring-detection)): Let $R$ be a unital associative ring spectrum and $k\ge0$. Then $$T(k)\wedge R\ne0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad K(k)\wedge R\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: For a unital associative ring spectrum, nonvanishing after smashing with the telescope implies nonvanishing after smashing with Morava K-theory, via the ring form of the nilpotence theorem.
- **claim-rational-unit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-unit)): If $k>0$ and $B$ is a nonzero commutative algebra in $\mathrm{Sp}_{K(k)}$, then its unit has nonzero image in $\pi_0B\otimes\mathbb Q$.
  - Explanation: The unit of a nonzero commutative algebra in the K(k)-local category stays nonzero in rational homotopy, by torsion nilpotence.
- **claim-abelian-full-mark** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-abelian-full-mark)): Let $B$ be a nonzero commutative $E$-algebra in $\mathrm{Sp}_{K(k)}$. For every $r\ge1$, put $C=(C_{p^r})^k$. Then $$\bigl(U_C(B)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: Surjective characters survive base change: for a product of cyclic groups of equal exponent, the rationalized cofree fixed points of any nonzero commutative Lubin-Tate algebra are nonzero.
- **claim-product-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-product-comparison)): For finite groups $P,C$ and an ordinary commutative ring spectrum $D$, there is a natural unit-preserving map $$U_{P\times C}(D)\longrightarrow U_C(U_P(D)),$$ where each displayed cofree construction uses the trivial action on its coefficient algebra.
  - Explanation: There is a natural unit-preserving map from cofree fixed points for a product group to the iterated construction for the two factors.
- **claim-height-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-height-gluing)): For a finite $p$-group $P$ and integers $k,l\ge0$, $$\label{eq:height-gluing} T(k)\wedge U_P(L_{T(l)}S)\ne0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad k+\ell(P,1)\le l.$$
  - Explanation: Nonvanishing of the telescope at one height against the cofree fixed points of a telescopic sphere forces the sum of that height and the cyclic length to be at most the telescopic height.
- **claim-finite-gluing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-gluing)): For every finite $p$-group $P$ and all $m,k\ge0$, $$\label{eq:finite-gluing} m<k+\ell(P,1)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad T(k)\wedge U_P(L_m^fS)=0.$$
  - Explanation: For the finite localization, the telescope smash with the cofree fixed points vanishes when the localization height is below the test height plus the cyclic length, proved by induction using the fracture square.
- **claim-layer-adjunction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-adjunction)): The functor $\phi_J$ is strong symmetric monoidal and has a fully faithful right adjoint $F_J$. Write $\mathcal A_J=F_J(\mathbf 1_{\mathcal D_J})$. Then:* 1. *The underlying geometric fixed points of its unit value are $$\label{eq:layer-unit} \Phi^K \mathcal A_J\simeq \bigoplus_{\substack{J'\sim J\\J'\trianglelefteq K}} U_{K/J'}(E).$$ For arbitrary $M\in\mathcal D_J$, choose, for each normal conjugate $J'=gJg^{-1}\trianglelefteq K$, the diagram $M_g$ obtained by restricting $M$ along $$K/J'\longrightarrow W_J,\qquad xJ'\longmapsto g^{-1}xgJ.$$ The formula for $\Phi^K F_JM$ has the same index set, with summand $\Phi^{K/J'}\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_{K/J'}(M_g)$. In particular, $\phi_KF_JM=0$ if no conjugate of $J$ is normal in $K$.* 2. *For every dualizable $z\in\mathcal C$ and every $M\in\mathcal D_J$, the projection map is an equivalence $$\label{eq:layer-projection} z\otimes F_JM\simeq F_J(\phi_Jz\otimes M).$$* 3. *The object $\mathcal A_J$ is a commutative algebra, and $F_JM$ is a unital $\mathcal A_J$-module.
  - Explanation: Geometric fixed points at a subgroup layer are strong symmetric monoidal with a fully faithful right adjoint satisfying a projection formula and an explicit fixed-point formula; its unit value is a commutative algebra.
- **claim-layer-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-splitting)): Let $z\in\mathcal C$ be dualizable and let $B\le G$. Suppose that $$\begin{aligned} \phi_K(z\otimes \mathcal A_B)&=0 &&(K\not\sim B),\label{eq:outgoing-orthogonality}\\ \phi_B(z\otimes \mathcal A_J)&=0 &&(J\not\sim B).\label{eq:incoming-orthogonality} \end{aligned}$$ Then the adjunction unit splits as $$z\simeq X\oplus Q,\qquad Q=F_B\phi_Bz\simeq z\otimes \mathcal A_B,$$ with $\phi_BX=0$, $\phi_KQ=0$ for $K\not\sim B$, and $X\otimes Q=0$. If $z$ is compact, both summands are compact.
  - Explanation: If a dualizable object is orthogonal to the other layer algebras in both directions, the adjunction unit splits it into an isolated layer summand and a complement, both compact when the object is compact.
- **claim-layer-orthogonality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-orthogonality)): Let $z\in\mathcal C_m^G$ be the image of a finite genuine $G$-spectrum, and let $n\le m$. Suppose that $B\le G$ satisfies $$m-n<\ell(G,B),$$ and that every $(K,k)\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(z)$ with $k<m$ satisfies $$\ell(G,K)\le k-n.$$ Then $z$ and $B$ satisfy both orthogonality conditions in [lem:layer-splitting](#lem:layer-splitting).
  - Explanation: When a subgroup violates the length bound and the support satisfies the bound at lower heights, finite gluing gives both orthogonality conditions needed for splitting.
- **claim-specialization-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-specialization)): For a finite $p$-group $G$, a subgroup $H\le G$, and $m,n\ge0$, $$(H,m)\in\overline{\{(G,n)\}} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad m\ge n+\ell(G,H).$$ The closure is taken in the tensor-triangular spectrum of finite genuine $p$-local $G$-spectra.
  - Explanation: If a subgroup-height point lies in the closure of a given point, its height is at least the starting height plus the cyclic length; a violation would split the irreducible support of a compact object.
- **claim-general-groups-inclusion** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-general-groups)): Let $G$ be any finite group, $p$ a prime, $H,K\le G$, and $m,n\ge0$. Then $$\mathcal P_m^G(K)\subseteq\mathcal P_n^G(H)$$ if and only if there is a conjugate $K^g=gKg^{-1}\le H$ such that $$N=O^p(H)=O^p(K^g),\qquad m\ge n+\ell(H/N,K^g/N).$$ In particular, the numerical test is applied to a qualifying conjugate in the finite $p$-group $H/N$.
  - Explanation: For any finite group, inclusion between primes holds exactly when a conjugate shares the p-residual and the numerical bound holds in the quotient p-group.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-relative-weights",
        "exact_quote": "In the coordinates <a href=\"#eq:groupoid-coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:groupoid-coordinates\">[eq:groupoid-coordinates]</a>, the $D$-linear extension of integration $\\epsilon_Y:A_Y\\to\\mathbf 1$ is the map $$\\label{eq:absolute-weights}\n (x_\\alpha)_{[\\alpha]}\\longmapsto\n       \\sum_{[\\alpha]}\\frac{x_\\alpha}{a_\\alpha}.$$ For a map $f:Y\\to Z$, its pushforward has coordinate formula $$\\label{eq:relative-weights}\n \\bigl(f_!(x)\\bigr)_\\beta\n   =\\sum_{[\\alpha]:[f\\alpha]=[\\beta]}\n       \\frac{a_\\beta}{a_\\alpha}\\,x_\\alpha.$$ Consequently $\\chi_\\beta(f_!1)$ is the rational scalar $$\\label{eq:relative-fiber-mass}\n \\left|\n   \\mathcal X_\\Gamma(Y)\n       \\mathop{\\times}^{h}_{\\mathcal X_\\Gamma(Z)}\\{\\beta\\}\n \\right|_{\\mathrm h}$$ times the unit of $D$.",
        "source_id": "PM-314.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-314.1"

      "exact_statement": "In the coordinates <a href=\"#eq:groupoid-coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:groupoid-coordinates\">[eq:groupoid-coordinates]</a>, the $D$-linear extension of integration $\\epsilon_Y:A_Y\\to\\mathbf 1$ is the map $$\\label{eq:absolute-weights}\n (x_\\alpha)_{[\\alpha]}\\longmapsto\n       \\sum_{[\\alpha]}\\frac{x_\\alpha}{a_\\alpha}.$$ For a map $f:Y\\to Z$, its pushforward has coordinate formula $$\\label{eq:relative-weights}\n \\bigl(f_!(x)\\bigr)_\\beta\n   =\\sum_{[\\alpha]:[f\\alpha]=[\\beta]}\n       \\frac{a_\\beta}{a_\\alpha}\\,x_\\alpha.$$ Consequently $\\chi_\\beta(f_!1)$ is the rational scalar $$\\label{eq:relative-fiber-mass}\n \\left|\n   \\mathcal X_\\Gamma(Y)\n       \\mathop{\\times}^{h}_{\\mathcal X_\\Gamma(Z)}\\{\\beta\\}\n \\right|_{\\mathrm h}$$ times the unit of $D$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-profinite-elementary",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a pro-$p$ group.*\n\n1.  *Every finite section of $E$ is a section of a finite quotient of $E$. If all finite sections of $E$ have generator number at most $l$, then every closed subgroup of $E$ is topologically generated by at most $l$ elements.*\n\n2.  *A procyclic pro-$p$ group is either trivial, finite cyclic, or isomorphic to $\\mathbb Z_p$. Every continuous epimorphism $K\\twoheadrightarrow\n    \\mathbb Z_p$ splits.*\n\n3.  *A finitely generated pro-$p$ group with infinite abelianization has a continuous epimorphism onto $\\mathbb Z_p$.",
        "source_id": "PM-314.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Let $E$ be a pro-$p$ group.*\n\n1.  *Every finite section of $E$ is a section of a finite quotient of $E$.         "exact_quote": "For $d\\geq1$, let $a_j(d)$ be the number of open subgroups of index $p^j$ in $\\mathbb Z_p^d$. Then $$\\label{eq:lattice-generating-function}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\sum_{j\\geq0}a_j(d)t^j\n       &=\\prod_{u=0}^{d-1}(1-p^u t)^{-1},\\\\\n \\lim_{j\\to\\infty}a_j(d)\n       &=c_d:=\\prod_{u=1}^{d-1}(1-p^u)^{-1}\\ne0\n \\quad\\text{in }\\mathbb Z_p.\n \\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-314.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-314.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-adjunction",
        "exact_quote": "The functor $\\phi_J$ is strong symmetric monoidal and has a fully faithful right adjoint $F_J$. Write $\\mathcal A_J=F_J(\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal D_J})$. Then:*\n\n\n1.  *The underlying geometric fixed points of its unit value are $$\\label{eq:layer-unit}\n     \\Phi^K \\mathcal A_J\\simeq\n     \\bigoplus_{\\substack{J'\\sim J\\\\J'\\trianglelefteq K}}\n     U_{K/J'}(E).$$ For arbitrary $M\\in\\mathcal D_J$, choose, for each normal conjugate $J'=gJg^{-1}\\trianglelefteq K$, the diagram $M_g$ obtained by restricting $M$ along $$K/J'\\longrightarrow W_J,\\qquad xJ'\\longmapsto g^{-1}xgJ.$$ The formula for $\\Phi^K F_JM$ has the same index set, with summand $\\Phi^{K/J'}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bor}}_{K/J'}(M_g)$. In particular, $\\phi_KF_JM=0$ if no conjugate of $J$ is normal in $K$.*\n\n\n2.  *For every dualizable $z\\in\\mathcal C$ and every $M\\in\\mathcal D_J$, the projection map is an equivalence $$\\label{eq:layer-projection}\n     z\\otimes F_JM\\simeq F_J(\\phi_Jz\\otimes M).$$*\n\n3.  *The object $\\mathcal A_J$ is a commutative algebra, and $F_JM$ is a unital $\\mathcal A_J$-module.",
        "source_id": "PM-314.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-adjunction",
          "exact_quote": "The functor $\\phi_J$ is strong symmetric monoidal and has a fully faithful right adjoint $F_J$. Write $\\mathcal A_J=F_J(\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal D_J})$. Then:*\n\n\n1.  *The underlying geometric fixed points of its unit value are $$\\label{eq:layer-unit}\n     \\Phi^K \\mathcal A_J\\simeq\n     \\bigoplus_{\\substack{J'\\sim J\\\\J'\\trianglelefteq K}}\n     U_{K/J'}(E).$$ For arbitrary $M\\in\\mathcal D_J$, choose, for each normal conjugate $J'=gJg^{-1}\\trianglelefteq K$, the diagram $M_g$ obtained by restricting $M$ along $$K/J'\\longrightarrow W_J,\\qquad xJ'\\longmapsto g^{-1}xgJ.$$ The formula for $\\Phi^K F_JM$ has the same index set, with summand $\\Phi^{K/J'}\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bor}}_{K/J'}(M_g)$. In particular, $\\phi_KF_JM=0$ if no conjugate of $J$ is normal in $K$.*\n\n\n2.  *For every dualizable $z\\in\\mathcal C$ and every $M\\in\\mathcal D_J$, the projection map is an equivalence $$\\label{eq:layer-projection}\n     z\\otimes F_JM\\simeq F_J(\\phi_Jz\\otimes M).$$*\n\n3.  *The object $\\mathcal A_J$ is a commutative algebra, and $F_JM$ is a unital $\\mathcal A_J$-module.",
          "source_id": "PM-314.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "The functor $\\phi_J$ is strong symmetric monoidal and has a fully faithful right adjoint $F_J$. Write $\\mathcal A_J=F_J(\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal D_J})$. In particular, $\\phi_KF_JM=0$ if no conjugate of $J$ is normal in $K$.*\n\n\n2.  *For every dualizable $z\\in\\mathcal C$ and every $M\\in\\mathcal D_J$, the projection map is an equivalence $$\\label{eq:layer-projection}\n     z\\otimes F_JM\\simeq F_J(\\phi_Jz\\otimes M).$$*\n\n3.  *The object $\\mathcal A_J$ is a commutative algebra, and $F_JM$ is a unital $\\mathcal A_J$-module.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-general-groups",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be any finite group, $p$ a prime, $H,K\\le G$, and $m,n\\ge0$. Then $$\\mathcal P_m^G(K)\\subseteq\\mathcal P_n^G(H)$$ if and only if there is a conjugate $K^g=gKg^{-1}\\le H$ such that $$N=O^p(H)=O^p(K^g),\\qquad\n m\\ge n+\\ell(H/N,K^g/N).$$ In particular, the numerical test is applied to a qualifying conjugate in the finite $p$-group $H/N$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **chromatic fixed-point loss r_n(G,H)** ([source](#sec:intro)): For a finite $p$-group $G$, a subgroup $H\le G$, and an integer $n\ge0$, define $r_n(G,H)$ to be the least nonnegative integer $r$ such that $$\label{eq:def-loss} K(n+r)_*(\Phi^H X)=0 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad K(n)_*(\Phi^G X)=0$$ for every finite $p$-local genuine $G$-spectrum $X$.
- **cyclic subnormal length** ([source](#sec:intro)): Let $\ell(G,H)$ be the least length of a subnormal chain $$H=G_0\trianglelefteq G_1\trianglelefteq\cdots \trianglelefteq G_s=G$$ whose successive quotients are cyclic.
- **telescope T(i)** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $i>0$, choose a finite type-$i$ spectrum with a periodic self-map and write $T(i)$ for its telescope.
- **type of a finite spectrum** ([source](#pm-h-6)): The type of a nonzero finite spectrum is its least nonvanishing Morava height.
- **finite localization L_m^f** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Write $L_m^f$ for localization at $T(0)\vee\cdots\vee T(m)$.
- **inflation** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Inflation of an ordinary spectrum $D$ will be denoted by $\operatorname{inf}_G D$.
- **cofree fixed-point functor U_P** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a finite $p$-group $P$ and a spectrum $D$, write $$U_P(D)=\Phi^P\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(D),$$ where $\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(D)$ is the genuine cofree spectrum associated to the trivial $P$-action on $D$.
- **I-mark** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The $I$-mark of a finite $F$-set $X$ is $\|X^I\|$.
- **Burnside idempotent e_I^F** ([source](#pm-h-8)): We denote by $e_I^F$ the idempotent with mark one at the conjugacy class of $I$ and zero at all others.
- **cochain algebra A_Y** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For a finite groupoid $Y$, put $A_Y=\mathbf 1^Y$, and abbreviate $A_{BF}$ to $A_F$.
- **occurring group** ([source](#pm-h-11)): A finite group $L$ *occurs* if $$D_L:=e_L^L A_L\ne0.$$
- **finite section** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A finite section of $\Gamma$ means a finite continuous quotient of a closed subgroup.
- **continuous mapping groupoid** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a profinite group $\Gamma$ and a finite groupoid $Y$, let $$\mathcal X_\Gamma(Y)=\mathop{\mathrm{Fun}}_{\mathrm{cts}}(B\Gamma,Y).$$
- **minimum number of generators d(F)** ([source](#sec:profinite)): For a finite $p$-group $F$, write $d(F)$ for its minimum number of generators.
- **subgroup congruences** ([source](#sec:profinite)): We say that a finitely generated pro-$p$ group *satisfies the subgroup congruences* when this formula holds for all its open subgroups.
- **p-residual O^p(J)** ([source](#pm-h-30)): For a finite group $J$, let $O^p(J)$ denote its smallest normal subgroup of $p$-power index.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-loss-equals-length | optimal chromatic fixed-point loss r_n(G,H) | = | $\ell(G,H)$ | every prime, subgroup and height |
| q-cyclic-upper | loss upper bound by cyclic subnormal length | ≤ | $\ell(G,H)$ | every subgroup and height |
| q-trivial-length | cyclic subnormal length of a group over itself | = | $0$ | H equal to G |
| q-example-loss-first-subgroup | loss for the first embedded subgroup of order p in the product of a cyclic group of order p squared and a cyclic group of order p | = | $2$ | every target height |
| q-example-loss-second-subgroup | loss for the second embedded subgroup of order p in the same group | = | $1$ | every target height |
| q-section-generator-bound | generator number of a section of an occurring group | ≤ | $l$ | every section A |
| q-congruence-valuation | p-adic valuation of the weighted fixed-point deviation sum over open subgroups of index p^h | ≥ | $h+1$ | every open subgroup U, finite continuous U-set X, h at least zero |
| q-procyclic-series-length | length t of the closed series with Z_p factors | ≤ | $l$ | l at least zero |
| q-lattice-limit-constant | p-adic limit c_d of the number of open subgroups of index p^j in Z_p^d | = | $\prod_{u=1}^{d-1}(1-p^u)^{-1}$ | d at least one |
| q-rational-gluing-bound | cyclic subnormal length of P over the trivial group when rational nonvanishing holds | ≤ | $l$ | every l at least zero |
| q-full-mark-trace | trace of the full-mark idempotent on cochains of the cyclic product | = | $p^{k^2(r-1)}\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}(p^k-p^i)$ | r at least one |
| q-height-gluing-bound | test height plus cyclic length when the telescope smash is nonzero | ≤ | $l$ | integers k and l at least zero |
| q-finite-gluing-vanishing-threshold | localization height m below which the telescope smash vanishes | < | $k+\ell(P,1)$ | all m and k at least zero |
| q-specialization-height | height m of a specialization of the point (G,n) | ≥ | $n+\ell(G,H)$ | every subgroup H and heights m, n at least zero |
| q-general-inclusion-height | height m in the prime inclusion test for arbitrary finite groups | ≥ | $n+\ell(H/N,K^g/N)$ | conjugates of K lying in H |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cyclic-upper** [claim](#stmt-prop-cyclic-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cyclic-upper): Apply the cyclic chromatic Smith theorem to each successive quotient of a cyclic subnormal chain and iterate over heights. -> loss at most the cyclic subnormal length
- **step-finite-local** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-local): Identify the finite localization with the compact quotient and apply the Balmer-Sanders point classification and localization theorem. -> finite spectrum with subgroup-height points and detection criteria
- **step-occurrence-fixed-points** [claim](#stmt-lem-occurrence-fixed-points) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-occurrence-fixed-points): Isotropy separation for the family of proper subgroups applied to the cofree spectrum identifies the full-mark summand with rational geometric fixed points. -> occurrence equivalent to nonvanishing of rational cofree fixed points
- **step-occurrence-sections** [claim](#stmt-prop-occurrence-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-occurrence-sections): Use Sylow units, pullback along quotients, a norm map for subgroups, and elementary abelian sharpness with the Frattini quotient. -> occurring groups are p-groups closed under sections with bounded generators
- **step-pro-characters** [claim](#stmt-thm-pro-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-pro-characters): Build a tower by maximizing conjugacy classes of homomorphisms, then split cochains over graph summands of the Burnside idempotent. -> pro-p group with bounded sections and evaluation splitting
- **step-cover-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-lem-cover-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-evaluation): Base change identifies the pulled-back transfer with a Burnside class whose full mark is selected by the idempotent. -> evaluation of covering transfers
- **step-relative-weights** [claim](#stmt-prop-relative-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-relative-weights): Evaluate the transfer of the unit along the diagonal and use composition of integrations to read off the weights. -> weighted integration and pushforward formulas
- **step-scalar-integrality** [claim](#stmt-lem-scalar-integrality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scalar-integrality): Show one plus p times an element is a unit using the mod-p Moore spectrum, then argue with a unit cochain. -> scalars are p-locally integral
- **step-hom-integrality** [claim](#stmt-lem-hom-integrality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hom-integrality): Integrate the unit over the classifying groupoid and apply scalar integrality. -> homomorphism count ratios are p-locally integral
- **step-subgroup-congruence** [claim](#stmt-thm-subgroup-congruence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-subgroup-congruence): Count labeled sets by integral pushforwards, pass to transitive sets through a logarithm of the generating series, and read off the congruence. -> subgroup congruences for open subgroups
- **step-profinite-elementary** [claim](#stmt-lem-profinite-elementary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profinite-elementary): Compactness gives topological generation, structure of closed subgroups of Z_p gives the classification, and module structure gives abelianization. -> elementary pro-p facts
- **step-shrinking-action** [claim](#stmt-lem-shrinking-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shrinking-action): Compare open subgroups of the direct product and of the twisted products through a common finite permutation quotient. -> congruences and generator bound pass to the kernel times larger Z_p power
- **step-lattice-limit** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lattice-limit): Recursive count of sublattices gives the generating function and p-adic convergence of partial sums. -> explicit generating function and nonzero p-adic limit
- **step-kernel-abelianization** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-abelianization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-abelianization): Count subgroups projecting onto the kernel in two ways; the congruence forces the count to tend to zero while the lattice limit forces a nonzero limit. -> infinite abelianization of the kernel
- **step-procyclic-series** [claim](#stmt-thm-procyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-abelianization-2): Peel off Z_p quotients repeatedly, shrinking the action each time, until the kernel is trivial; the section bound limits the number of steps. -> closed series with Z_p factors of bounded length
- **step-rational-gluing** [claim](#stmt-cor-rational-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-rational-gluing): At positive height combine the tower, the integrality results and the procyclic theorem; at height zero use rational averaging. -> rational nonvanishing bounds the cyclic length
- **step-product-series** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-series) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-series): Choose compatible chains by compactness, pass to the inverse limit, and count the steps that raise the free rank. -> free rank subtracts from the length bound
- **step-ring-detection** [claim](#stmt-lem-ring-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ring-detection): Apply the ring form of the nilpotence theorem to the periodic self-map in the endomorphism ring smash. -> telescope nonvanishing detected by Morava K-theory
- **step-rational-unit** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-unit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-unit): Torsion nilpotence in the semiadditive category shows a torsion unit would force the algebra to vanish. -> unit nonzero rationally
- **step-abelian-full-mark** [claim](#stmt-prop-abelian-full-mark) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-abelian-full-mark): Compute the trace of the full-mark projector over Lubin-Tate theory by generalized characters, then base change to the algebra. -> nonzero rational cofree fixed points for the cyclic product
- **step-product-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-comparison): Compare representation spheres to relate mixed restriction with geometric fixed points and use the cofree model with transitivity. -> unit-preserving comparison map for product groups
- **step-height-gluing** [claim](#stmt-thm-height-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-height-gluing): Move the telescope nonvanishing to a nonzero Lubin-Tate algebra, map through the product comparison, apply rational gluing to products with cyclic groups, then subtract rank. -> test height plus cyclic length bounded by the height
- **step-finite-gluing** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-gluing): Induct on the localization height using the fracture square and a retraction argument for modules. -> vanishing for finite localizations
- **step-layer-adjunction** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-adjunction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-adjunction): Construct the right adjoint through normal geometric localization and coinduction, compute fixed points by the orbit formula, and derive the projection formula. -> fully faithful right adjoint with projection formula
- **step-layer-splitting** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-splitting): Strip other layers one by one through fibre sequences and use orthogonality of mapping spectra to split the unit. -> splitting of a dualizable object at an isolated layer
- **step-layer-orthogonality** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-orthogonality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-orthogonality): Concatenate chains and apply finite gluing to each summand of the layer algebra, with detection by retained telescopes. -> both orthogonality conditions
- **step-specialization** [claim](#stmt-thm-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-specialization): Take a least violating height, build a compact object supported on the closure, split it by layer splitting and contradict irreducibility of a point closure. -> specialization bound
- **step-main-equality** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-specialization-2): Translate the defining implication into prime inclusion, apply the specialization bound for the lower bound and the cyclic upper bound for the other direction. -> equality of loss and cyclic length
- **step-general-groups** [claim](#stmt-cor-general-groups) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-general-groups): Reduce to p-subnormal pairs by the Balmer-Sanders result and apply the specialization and cyclic upper bounds in the quotient p-group. -> prime inclusion criterion for arbitrary finite groups

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-loss-equals-length**: For a finite p-group G, a subgroup H and every height n, the optimal chromatic fixed-point loss equals the shortest length of a subnormal chain from H to G with cyclic quotients. Hence the loss is independent of the height and is a purely group-theoretic quantity.
- **inv-cyclic-upper-bound**: The loss never exceeds the cyclic subnormal length; this is the half of the equality obtained by applying the cyclic chromatic Smith theorem along a chain.
- **inv-trivial-chain-length**: The cyclic subnormal length is defined for chains that exist in every finite p-group, and it is zero when the subgroup is the whole group.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-general-group-no-residual**: Evaluating prime inclusions for an arbitrary finite group by the raw cyclic chain length of the whole group. -> For non-p-groups the chain length is only meaningful after passing to the quotient by the common p-residual of a qualifying conjugate; skipping that reduction gives wrong inclusion tests.
- **anti-elementary-rank-substitute**: Replacing the cyclic chain length by the rank of the largest elementary abelian quotient in which H dies. -> For nonabelian groups this rank can be strictly smaller than the true loss, so the substitute underestimates it.
- **anti-weight-by-quotient-order**: Charging a cyclic step according to the order of its quotient instead of one unit per step. -> A cyclic quotient of large prime-power order would be counted several times and the computed length would be too big.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The right-hand side of the main equality is the least length of a subnormal chain with cyclic quotients, a finite combinatorial quantity of a finite group pair. The harness computes it exactly by breadth-first search over the subgroup lattice of small permutation groups, as the case where a finite procedure decides the group-theoretic side of the theorem. The topological loss itself is not computed.

```python
# INPUTS: one JSON object on stdin: degree n, generators of G as permutations of range(n) (G a finite 2-group), generators of H.
# PRECONDITIONS: H is a subgroup of G; G is small enough to enumerate all elements.
# OUTPUTS: one integer on stdout, the least length of a subnormal chain from H to G with cyclic quotients.
# TIME: polynomial in the group order per subgroup visited, over the subgroups reached by the search.
# SPACE: proportional to the number of subgroups visited times the group order.
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer and permutation arithmetic only, no floating point.
# FAILURE: exits with code 2 if H is not contained in G, code 3 if no chain is found.
import sys, json
from collections import deque

def mul(a, b):
    return tuple(a[b[i]] for i in range(len(a)))

def closure(gens, n):
    e = tuple(range(n))
    seen = {e}
    stack = [e]
    gens = [tuple(g) for g in gens]
    while stack:
        a = stack.pop()
        for g in gens:
            c = mul(g, a)
            if c not in seen:
                seen.add(c)
                stack.append(c)
    return seen

def inverse(a):
    r = [0] * len(a)
    for i, v in enumerate(a):
        r[v] = i
    return tuple(r)

def main():
    data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
    n = data["n"]
    G = frozenset(closure(data["G"], n))
    hg = [tuple(h) for h in data["H"]]
    start = frozenset(closure(hg, n))
    if not start <= G:
        sys.exit(2)
    inv = {x: inverse(x) for x in G}
    dist = {start: 0}
    queue = deque([start])
    while queue:
        S = queue.popleft()
        d = dist[S]
        if S == G:
            print(d)
            return
        for x in G:
            if x in S:
                continue
            if all(mul(mul(x, s), inv[x]) in S for s in S):
                K = frozenset(closure(list(S) + [x], n))
                nd = d + 1
                if K not in dist:
                    dist[K] = nd
                    queue.append(K)
    sys.exit(3)

main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys, json\ninp = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\ncand = open(sys.argv[2]).read().strip()\nexp = sys.argv[3].strip()\nn = inp[\"n\"]\nE = tuple(range(n))\n\ndef comp(p, q):\n    return tuple(p[q[i]] for i in range(n))\n\ndef gen(elems):\n    S = {E} | {tuple(x) for x in elems}\n    while True:\n        T = {comp(a, b) for a in S for b in S} | S\n        if T == S:\n            return frozenset(S)\n        S = T\n\nG = gen(inp[\"G\"])\nH = gen(inp[\"H\"])\ncyc = {gen([x]) for x in G}\nsubs = set(cyc)\nchanged = True\nwhile changed:\n    changed = False\n    for A in list(subs):\n        for C in cyc:\n            B = gen(A | C)\n            if B not in subs:\n                subs.add(B)\n                changed = True\n\ndef inv(a):\n    return next(b for b in G if comp(a, b) == E)\n\ndef cyc_quot(A, B):\n    m = len(B) // len(A)\n    for x in B:\n        y = x\n        k = 1\n        while y not in A:\n            y = comp(y, x)\n            k += 1\n        if k == m:\n            return True\n    return False\n\nedges = []\nfor A in subs:\n    for B in subs:\n        if A < B and all(comp(comp(b, a), inv(b)) in A for b in B for a in A) and cyc_quot(A, B):\n            edges.append((A, B))\nINF = 10 ** 9\ndist = {S: INF for S in subs}\ndist[H] = 0\nfor _ in range(len(subs)):\n    for A, B in edges:\n        if dist[A] + 1 < dist[B]:\n            dist[B] = dist[A] + 1\ntruth = dist[G]\nok = (truth < INF and cand.lstrip('-').isdigit() and int(cand) == truth\n      and exp.lstrip('-').isdigit() and int(exp) == truth)\nsys.exit(0 if ok else 1)\n",

The harness computes it exactly by breadth-first search over the subgroup lattice of small permutation groups, as the case where a finite procedure decides the group-theoretic side of the theorem. The topological loss itself is not computed.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: one JSON object on stdin: degree n, generators of G as permutations of range(n) (G a finite 2-group), generators of H.\n# PRECONDITIONS: H is a subgroup of G; G is small enough to enumerate all elements.\n# OUTPUTS: one integer on stdout, the least length of a subnormal chain from H to G with cyclic quotients.\n# TIME: polynomial in the group order per subgroup visited, over the subgroups reached by the search.\n# SPACE: proportional to the number of subgroups visited times the group order.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact integer and permutation arithmetic only, no floating point.\n# FAILURE: exits with code 2 if H is not contained in G, code 3 if no chain is found.\nimport sys, json\nfrom collections import deque\n\ndef mul(a, b):\n    return tuple(a[b[i]] for i in range(len(a)))\n\ndef closure(gens, n):\n    e = tuple(range(n))\n    seen = {e}\n    stack = [e]\n    gens = [tuple(g) for g in gens]\n    while stack:\n        a = stack.pop()\n        for g in gens:\n            c = mul(g, a)\n            if c not in seen:\n                seen.add(c)\n                stack.append(c)\n    return seen\n\ndef inverse(a):\n    r = [0] * len(a)\n    for i, v in enumerate(a):\n        r[v] = i\n    return tuple(r)\n\ndef main():\n    data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\n    n = data[\"n\"]\n    G = frozenset(closure(data[\"G\"], n))\n    hg = [tuple(h) for h in data[\"H\"]]\n    start = frozenset(closure(hg, n))\n    if not start <= G:\n        sys.exit(2)\n    inv = {x: inverse(x) for x in G}\n    dist = {start: 0}\n    queue = deque([start])\n    while queue:\n        S = queue.popleft()\n        d = dist[S]\n        if S == G:\n            print(d)\n            return\n        for x in G:\n            if x in S:\n                continue\n            if all(mul(mul(x, s), inv[x]) in S for s in S):\n                K = frozenset(closure(list(S) + [x], n))\n                nd = d + 1\n                if K not in dist:\n                    dist[K] = nd\n                    queue.append(K)\n    sys.exit(3)\n\nmain()\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves a theorem in equivariant stable homotopy theory and defines no interface, data format or program. A library could only wrap the group-theoretic quantity, and the paper does not describe one. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamical system, physical model or discretisation appears. Genuine G-spectra are not simulable objects. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no tool and no procedure that a user would run. The theorem's usable content is a numerical identity, which is better treated under algorithms. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The theorem reduces the topological quantity to the least length of a subnormal chain with cyclic quotients in a finite p-group. That is a finite search over the subgroup lattice, so a shortest-chain computation is a plausible adaptation. The paper itself gives no algorithm. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No data structure is introduced. The subgroup lattice is classical background and plays no role as a structure with operations or invariants in the paper. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns circuits, memory layout or parallel execution. All objects are abstract spectra and profinite groups. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction is present. The p-adic and group-counting arguments are used only to prove structure of a profinite group. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, data distribution or model class in the paper. The only inference is between vanishing statements on homology of finite spectra. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The least chain length is a minimisation over finite chains, but the paper treats it as an invariant in a theorem, with no objective, constraints or solver. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no processes, messages, failures or consensus. Equivariance under a finite group is a symmetry notion, not a distributed model. |
| other (pure mathematics: equivariant chromatic homotopy and tensor-triangular geometry) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The result settles the chromatic Smith problem for finite p-groups and determines the remaining prime inclusions in the Balmer spectrum of finite genuine equivariant spectra, for arbitrary finite groups through reduction to p-subnormal pairs. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-p-group-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The equality of loss and cyclic subnormal length is stated only for finite p-groups, every prime, every subgroup and every nonnegative height. Nothing is claimed for groups that are not p-groups until the common p-residual reduction is applied. Blocked use: ['Applying the equality directly to an arbitrary finite group or to a profinite or compact Lie group', 'Reading the cyclic chain length of a non-p-group as the chromatic loss']
- **limit-general-group-reduction** (formal_scope): For an arbitrary finite group the numerical test is applied only to a qualifying conjugate inside the quotient p-group by the common p-residual. Blocked use: ['Testing prime inclusion in a finite group by a raw chain length in that group, without a qualifying conjugate and the residual quotient']
- **limit-embedding-matters** (edge_case): The loss depends on how H sits inside G, not only on the isomorphism type of H. Two isomorphic subgroups can have different losses. Blocked use: ['Computing the loss from the isomorphism type of H alone', 'Replacing the embedded pair (G,H) by an abstract quotient order or by the rank of a quotient']
- **limit-cyclic-step-unit-cost** (edge_case): Every cyclic quotient counts as exactly one step, whatever its order. A length computation that weights steps by quotient order is not the quantity in the theorem. Blocked use: ['Weighting chain steps by the order of the cyclic quotient', 'Using a total order or exponent count as a stand-in for the chain length']
- **limit-complete-universe-finite-groups** (formal_scope): The framework uses genuine G-spectra on a complete universe for finite groups, in the p-local setting. The results do not cover other universes or infinite acting groups. Blocked use: ['Transferring the equality to actions of the circle group or other compact Lie groups', 'Using incomplete-universe equivariant categories without a separate argument']
- **limit-positive-height-tower-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The character construction is carried out at a fixed prime and a positive height, with the telescopic sphere as unit. The height zero case is handled by a different, separate argument. Blocked use: ['Applying the pro-p tower and evaluation splitting at height zero', 'Using the integrality and congruence results without fixing a positive height']
- **limit-height-zero-separate** (edge_case): At height zero the rational gluing estimate is proved by rational averaging: for a nontrivial group the full-mark summand vanishes. The tower argument is not used. Blocked use: ['Citing the tower and subgroup congruences as the proof at height zero', 'Assuming nonvanishing at height zero for a nontrivial p-group']
- **limit-tower-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The tower of occurring groups is built by choosing maximizing extensions at each stage, and the source gives no explicit termination or stage bound. The pro-p group is therefore not produced by a finite procedure. Blocked use: ['Using the construction as an algorithm that outputs the pro-p group or an explicit stage', 'Quoting a computable bound on the stage needed for the evaluation splitting']
- **limit-lower-bound-by-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The lower bound comes from a contradiction with irreducibility of the closure of a point, not from an explicit witness. The sharpness spectra are obtained by separation of primes, and no explicit cell structure is given. Blocked use: ['Reading the proof as a recipe for building a finite spectrum that realizes the loss', 'Using the paper to synthesize explicit cell attachments for separating spectra']
- **limit-chain-existence** (edge_case): Chains with cyclic quotients exist in every finite p-group, and the length is zero when H equals G. A chain search must treat the case H equal to G as length zero rather than as a failure. Blocked use: ['Treating H equal to G as an error or as length one', 'Running a chain search on a group that is not a p-group and expecting existence']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For a finite <i>p</i>-group <i>G</i> and a subgroup <i>H</i>, we prove that the optimal chromatic fixed-point loss equals the shortest length of a subnormal chain from <i>H</i> to <i>G</i> with cyclic quotients. The equality holds at every prime and every nonnegative height, resolving positively the equality proposed by Kuhn and Lloyd.

<a id="sec:intro"></a>
### Introduction

The chromatic Smith problem asks how much information about fixed points at one subgroup is forced by chromatic acyclicity at another. Fix a prime $p$, and let $K(i)$ denote Morava $K$-theory at $p$, with $K(0)=H\mathbb Q$. For a finite $p$-group $G$, a subgroup $H\le G$, and an integer $n\ge0$, define $r_n(G,H)$ to be the least nonnegative integer $r$ such that <a id="eq:def-loss"></a>$$\label{eq:def-loss}
 K(n+r)_*(\Phi^H X)=0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 K(n)_*(\Phi^G X)=0$$ for every finite $p$-local genuine $G$-spectrum $X$. Here $\Phi^H X$ denotes the underlying spectrum of the geometric fixed points, and finite spectra include compact retracts.

Let $\ell(G,H)$ be the least length of a subnormal chain $$H=G_0\trianglelefteq G_1\trianglelefteq\cdots
 \trianglelefteq G_s=G$$ whose successive quotients are cyclic. We set $\ell(G,G)=0$. Such chains exist in finite $p$-groups. The cyclic chromatic Smith theorem, applied along a chain, gives $r_n(G,H)\le\ell(G,H)$. Our result is the converse.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every prime $p$, finite $p$-group $G$, subgroup $H\le G$, and integer $n\ge0$, $$\boxed{r_n(G,H)=\ell(G,H).}$$*

</div>

Thus the optimal loss is independent of the height. For every integer $0\le r<\ell(G,H)$, there is a finite genuine $G$-spectrum whose geometric $H$-fixed points are $K(n+r)$-acyclic and whose geometric $G$-fixed points have nonzero $K(n)$-homology. The theorem includes rational fixed-point detection at $n=0$.

The embedding of $H$ matters. For example, in $G=C_{p^2}\times C_p$, take $H_1=pC_{p^2}\times0$ and $H_2=0\times C_p$. Both subgroups are isomorphic to $C_p$, but $G/H_1\cong C_p\times C_p$ and $G/H_2\cong C_{p^2}$. Chains above $H_i$ correspond to chains in these quotients. The first quotient requires two cyclic steps, while the second requires one. Thus $r_n(G,H_1)=2$ and $r_n(G,H_2)=1$ at every target height. A cyclic quotient of order $p^2$ still counts only once.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and significance

The classical starting point is Smith theory: for a finite complex with an action of a finite $p$-group, mod-$p$ acyclicity passes to fixed points. Floyd strengthened this to an inequality between total mod-$p$ Betti numbers <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Floyd](#ref-Floyd), Theorem 4.4]; the finite $p$-group statement follows by iteration from prime-order actions. Chromatic Smith theory asks which implications survive when mod-$p$ homology is replaced by individual Morava theories.

Building on the nonequivariant thick subcategory theorem of Hopkins and Smith <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HS](#ref-HS)], Balmer and Sanders identified all primes of the category of finite genuine equivariant spectra. They reduced its topology to subgroup–height inclusions and computed the case $C_p$, crediting Strickland’s earlier work <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS), Introduction, Theorems 4.9 and 4.14, and Section 7]. Barthel, Hausmann, Naumann, Nikolaus, Noel, and Stapleton determined the finite abelian $p$-group case: the loss for $H\le G$ is the minimum number of generators of $G/H$ <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BHNNNS](#ref-BHNNNS), Theorem 2.3]. This replaced the original order-based prediction by a rank bound; already $C_{p^2}$ has loss one rather than two. The sharp elementary abelian examples use the partition-complex work of Arone, Dwyer, Lesh, and Mahowald <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BHNNNS](#ref-BHNNNS), Theorems 2.2 and 4.1]. Balderrama and Kuhn later gave an elementary proof of the abelian chromatic Smith theorem, including target height zero <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BK](#ref-BK), Theorem 1.1 and Section 3.2].

For nonabelian groups, a general lower bound is the rank of the largest elementary abelian quotient of $G$ in which $H$ maps to the identity. This can be smaller than the cyclic-chain upper bound. Kuhn and Lloyd proved that chromatic acyclicity implications are equivalent to corresponding inequalities between Morava homology dimensions, allowing representation-theoretic constructions to give stronger lower bounds <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KL](#ref-KL), Theorem 2.2]. They obtained equality with $\ell$ for every subgroup pair inside a central extension $C_2\to G\to E$ with $E$ elementary abelian <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[KL](#ref-KL), Theorem 2.13]. In particular, a noncentral subgroup of order two in $D_8$ has loss two, although the elementary-quotient lower bound is one. Their discussion of the general problem expresses “cautious hope” that $r_n(G,H)=r_+(G,H)=\ell(G,H)$, and separately asks whether the loss is independent of height <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KL](#ref-KL), Section 2.2]. [thm:main](#thm:main) establishes the proposed equality, and hence height independence, for all finite $p$-groups.

The remaining obstacle is sharpness for an arbitrary embedded subgroup. The Hopkins–Kuhn–Ravenel characters are indexed by maps from $\mathbb Z_p^n$, whose images are abelian <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HKR](#ref-HKR), Theorem C]. Thus they cannot directly supply characters with full nonabelian image. General character rings with other source groups already appear in Kuhn’s framework <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[KuhnCharacters](#ref-KuhnCharacters), Section 2]. Here we select a profinite source from the finite groups detected by telescopic cochains, derive constraints on all its open subgroups by relative integration, and use those constraints to prove the required fixed-point vanishing estimates.

Combined with the conjugacy and $p$-subnormal reductions of Balmer–Sanders, the theorem determines the remaining finite-height prime inclusions for arbitrary finite groups <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS), Proposition 6.11]; [cor:general-groups](#cor:general-groups) gives the precise test. Their description of the infinite-height inclusions and of Thomason subsets then determines the thick tensor ideals <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS), Corollaries 8.19, 10.5, and 10.6]. The sharpness witnesses in this paper are obtained by separation of primes.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof

<a id="eq:intro-rational"></a>
For a finite $p$-group $P$ and a spectrum $D$, write $$U_P(D)=\Phi^P\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(D),$$ where $\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(D)$ is the genuine cofree spectrum associated to the trivial $P$-action on $D$. Let $T(l)$ be a telescope at height $l$. The first main step is the rational estimate $$\label{eq:intro-rational}
 \bigl(U_P(L_{T(l)}S)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}\ne0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \ell(P,1)\le l.$$ To prove it at positive $l$, we rationalize morphisms in the tensor category of dualizable $T(l)$-local spectra and split Burnside idempotents. A maximizing construction produces a pro-$p$ group $\Gamma$ mapping onto $P$, with every finite section (a finite continuous quotient of a closed subgroup) generated by at most $l$ elements. Cochains admit evaluation factors indexed by continuous homomorphisms out of $\Gamma$. Height zero is handled separately by rational averaging.

The key additional information comes from relative integration over finite groupoids. Strickland’s Frobenius formulation of HKR character theory already expresses such integration through automorphism-group weights <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[StricklandDuality](#ref-StricklandDuality), Section 10 and Theorem 10.2]. We derive the weights for the source $\Gamma$ just constructed. Applying them to groupoids of labeled finite sets, coinduced from an open subgroup, gives congruences $$\sum_{\substack{V\le_o U\\{}[U:V]=p^h}}
 \bigl(|X^V|-|X|\bigr)
 \in p^{h+1}\mathbb Z_{(p)}$$ for every open subgroup $U\le\Gamma$, every finite continuous $U$-set $X$, and every $h\ge0$. The same construction gives $\lvert\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathrm{cts}}(\Gamma,F)\rvert/\lvert F\rvert\in\mathbb Z_{(p)}$ for every finite group $F$. Together with the finite-section bound, these arithmetic conditions force a closed subnormal series with $\mathbb Z_p$-factors and length at most $l$. The purely profinite argument uses shrinking semidirect products and an explicit $p$-adic limit of subgroup counts; it may be useful independently of the chromatic application.

<a id="eq:intro-height"></a>
Next, ring nilpotence and the generalized characters of Hopkins, Kuhn, and Ravenel <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HS](#ref-HS), [HKR](#ref-HKR)] upgrade <a href="#eq:intro-rational" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-rational">[eq:intro-rational]</a> to $$\label{eq:intro-height}
 T(k)\wedge U_P(L_{T(l)}S)\ne0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad k+\ell(P,1)\le l.$$ Chromatic ambidexterity and its torsion-nilpotence consequence <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CSY](#ref-CSY)] allow this step to use arbitrary nonzero commutative local algebras. A product with increasingly large cyclic $p$-groups separates the height $k$ from the cyclic length.

Finally, finite chromatic fracture converts <a href="#eq:intro-height" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-height">[eq:intro-height]</a> into vanishing estimates for the right adjoints to subgroup geometric fixed points. The support argument adapts the strategy of Balmer–Sanders <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS), proof of Theorem 9.1, (B)(A)]. At a least height where the proposed bound fails, the estimates isolate the offending subgroup component from the other components. In the finite-height tensor-triangular spectrum, this forces the support of a compact object to split into two nonempty disjoint closed parts, although that support is the closure of a point and hence irreducible. The contradiction supplies the lower bound for every embedded subgroup.

Published inputs are recalled in [sec:background](#sec:background); the character construction, congruences, profinite theorem, higher-height estimate, and support argument occupy [sec:characters](#sec:characters), [sec:congruences](#sec:congruences), [sec:profinite](#sec:profinite), [sec:gluing](#sec:gluing), [sec:support](#sec:support), respectively.

<a id="sec:background"></a>
### Chromatic and equivariant preliminaries

We work $p$-locally. The category $\mathrm{Sp}^G$ is the stable category of genuine $G$-spectra on a complete universe; its compact objects are retracts of $p$-local finite genuine $G$-CW spectra. All limits, tensor products, and fixed-point functors are derived. When a localized category is used, its tensor product is the corresponding localized smash product.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Chromatic tests and finite localization

<a id="eq:finite-tests"></a>
For $i>0$, choose a finite type-$i$ spectrum with a periodic self-map and write $T(i)$ for its telescope. Put $T(0)=K(0)=H\mathbb Q$. The Bousfield class of $T(i)$ is independent of the choice. For a finite spectrum $V$, $$\label{eq:finite-tests}
 T(i)\wedge V=0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad K(i)\wedge V=0.$$ The type of a nonzero finite spectrum is its least nonvanishing Morava height. Nilpotence and periodicity give these facts and finite spectra of every type <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[HS](#ref-HS)].

<a id="eq:fracture"></a>
Write $L_m^f$ for localization at $T(0)\vee\cdots\vee T(m)$. This is a smashing finite localization; its acyclic compact ideal is generated by a finite spectrum of type $m+1$. Consequently an $L_m^f$-local spectrum is zero if and only if its smashes with $T(0),\ldots,T(m)$ vanish. We use the telescopic fracture square, a homotopy pullback $$\label{eq:fracture}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large,row sep=large]
 L_m^fD \arrow[r]\arrow[d]&L_{T(m)}D\arrow[d]\\
 L_{m-1}^fD\arrow[r]&L_{m-1}^fL_{T(m)}D
\end{tikzcd}$$ for $m>0$ and $D$ already $L_m^f$-local; equivalently take $D=L_m^fS$ for the sphere square used below. The square follows from the complementary localizations at the top telescope and the lower finite heights: $L_{T(m)}L_{m-1}^f=0$, and the two localizations jointly detect $L_m^f$-local equivalences.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Genuine fixed points and cofree spectra

Inflation of an ordinary spectrum $D$ will be denoted by $\operatorname{inf}_G D$. It satisfies $$\Phi^H(\operatorname{inf}_G D)\simeq D
 \qquad(H\le G).$$ Geometric fixed points are exact and symmetric monoidal, preserve finite objects, and jointly detect equivalences when taken over all subgroups. For a normal subgroup, their genuine quotient versions are transitive.

The functor from genuine $P$-spectra to underlying $P$-diagrams has a cofree right adjoint. For the constant diagram with value $D$, put $$\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(D)=F(EP_+,\operatorname{inf}_P D),
 \qquad U_P(D)=\Phi^P\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(D).$$ We also use $\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P$ for that right adjoint on a general $P$-diagram, specifying the diagram when its action is nontrivial. Its categorical fixed points on a constant diagram are $D^{BP}=F(BP_+,D)$. The functor $U_P$ is exact and lax symmetric monoidal. In particular it takes commutative algebras and their modules to commutative algebras and their modules.

If $X$ is dualizable, adjunction gives the cofree projection formula <a id="eq:cofree-projection"></a>$$\label{eq:cofree-projection}
 X\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P(M)\simeq
 \mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_P\bigl(\operatorname{res}X\otimes M\bigr),$$ where $\operatorname{res}X$ retains its underlying $P$-action. We use the usual restriction, coinduction, and normal geometric-localization formulas for genuine spectra <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS), Section 2].

<a id="stmt-prop-cyclic-upper"></a>
<div id="prop:cyclic-upper" class="proposition">

**Proposition 2** (Cyclic upper bound). *For every finite $p$-group $G$, subgroup $H$, and $n\ge0$, $r_n(G,H)\le\ell(G,H)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-cyclic-upper"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-cyclic-upper</summary>

*Proof.* The finite abelian chromatic Smith theorem gives loss at most one for every nontrivial cyclic $p$-group, including target height zero <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BHNNNS](#ref-BHNNNS), Theorem 2.1 and Corollary 3.12]; see also <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KL](#ref-KL), Theorem 5.1]. Along a length-$s$ cyclic subnormal chain, apply it to the finite genuine $G_i/G_{i-1}$-spectrum obtained by restricting $X$ to $G_i$ and taking genuine geometric $G_{i-1}$-fixed points. Transitivity identifies the underlying and full fixed points with $\Phi^{G_{i-1}}X$ and $\Phi^{G_i}X$. Iterating at heights $n+s-1,\ldots,n$ proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Ambidexterity and Burnside marks

The relevant ambidexterity belongs to the development of chromatic Tate vanishing and higher semiadditivity. Kuhn proved telescopic finite-group Tate vanishing <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[KuhnTate](#ref-KuhnTate), Theorem 1.5]; Hopkins and Lurie established higher ambidexterity in the Morava-local category <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[HL](#ref-HL), Theorem 0.0.2]. The telescopic form below is due to Carmeli, Schlank, and Yanovski. None of these uses identifies telescopic localization with Morava localization on arbitrary spectra.

We use the published ambidexterity theorem for $\mathrm{Sp}_{T(l)}$, $\mathrm{Sp}_{K(l)}$, and $K(l)$-local $E_l$-modules <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CSY](#ref-CSY), Theorem 5.3.1 and Corollary 5.3.2]. In these categories every finite groupoid is ambidextrous. Integration along maps of finite groupoids satisfies composition, homotopy base change, and the projection formula; for finite coverings it is the ordinary sum transfer <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[CSY](#ref-CSY), Section 3.1]. Cochains on a finite groupoid with values in the unit are dualizable <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[CSY](#ref-CSY), Proposition 3.3.7]. Tensoring preserves finite-groupoid limits, since it preserves their ambidextrous colimits <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[CSY](#ref-CSY), Corollary 3.2.4]. Thus the canonical exterior product of unit-cochains is an algebra equivalence $$\mathbf 1^{BY}\otimes\mathbf 1^{BZ}\simeq\mathbf 1^{B(Y\times Z)}$$ for finite groups $Y,Z$, and similarly for finite groupoids.

For a finite group $F$, its rational Burnside ring is the product of copies of $\mathbb Q$ indexed by conjugacy classes of subgroups. The $I$-mark of a finite $F$-set $X$ is $|X^I|$. We denote by $e_I^F$ the idempotent with mark one at the conjugacy class of $I$ and zero at all others. A finite $F$-set $X$ acts on cochains by multiplication by the transfer of $1$ along $X\mathbin{//}F\to BF$; for an orbit, this is restriction followed by transfer. This agrees with the genuine Burnside action. The full mark $e_F^F$ will be used to isolate full geometric fixed points.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Prime points and finite truncation

<a id="eq:prime-closure"></a>
For the rest of the paper, primes are indexed by the *tested height*: $$\mathcal P_i^G(H)=
 \{X\in(\mathrm{Sp}^G)^c:K(i)_*(\Phi^H X)=0\},
 \qquad i\ge0.$$ Thus $\mathcal P_i^G(H)$ has index $i+1$ in the convention of <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS)]. We suppress $G$ when it is fixed. The support of a compact object $X$ consists of the primes not containing it. In particular $$\label{eq:prime-closure}
 q\in\overline{\{y\}}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q\subseteq y.$$

<a id="stmt-prop-finite-local"></a>
<div id="prop:finite-local" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Uniform finite localization). *Let $\mathcal C_m^G$ be the category of genuine $G$-spectra localized by the inflated sphere $L_m^fS$. It is rigidly compactly generated. Its compact tensor-triangular spectrum is the finite subspace $$\{(H,i):H\le G\text{ up to conjugacy},\ 0\le i\le m\}$$ of the equivariant spectrum, with $(H,i)$ representing $\mathcal P_i^G(H)$. The image of a finite genuine spectrum is compact and dualizable. An object $Z\in\mathcal C_m^G$ is zero if and only if $$T(i)\wedge\Phi^H Z=0
 \qquad(H\le G,\ 0\le i\le m).$$ For the image of a finite object, its support is tested equally by $T(i)$ and $K(i)$ at these heights. For every compact object of $\mathcal C_m^G$, membership in the support at $(H,i)$ is equivalent to nonvanishing of $K(i)\wedge\Phi^H(-)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-finite-local"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-finite-local</summary>

*Proof.* Inflation of a finite type-$(m+1)$ spectrum generates the kernel as a localizing tensor ideal. Equivalently, use its products with the finite orbit generators. This is a finite localization, and its compact category is the idempotent completion of the corresponding compact quotient. The point classification and localization theorem of Balmer–Sanders identify its spectrum with the displayed subspace <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BS](#ref-BS), Theorems 4.9 and 4.14, Remark 5.3, and Proposition 5.24]. Indeed the inflated generator belongs precisely to the primes at test heights at most $m$.

Geometric fixed points commute with this inflated localization. Each $\Phi^H Z$ is therefore $L_m^f$-local; telescope detection at the retained heights, followed by genuine geometric detection, proves the zero criterion. For a finite representative, <a href="#eq:finite-tests" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-tests">[eq:finite-tests]</a> gives agreement of the two support tests. For the assertion about all compact objects, the kernel of $K(i)\wedge\Phi^H(-)$ on compacts is a tensor-triangular prime by the Morava $K$-theory field property. Its inverse image in the original compact category is $\mathcal P_i^G(H)$, since $K(i)\wedge L_m^fS\simeq K(i)$ for $i\le m$. The injectivity of the spectrum map for finite localization therefore identifies this kernel with the point labelled $(H,i)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:characters"></a>
### Rational characters from finite groups

Fix a prime $p$ and a positive height $l$, and put $\mathbf 1=L_{T(l)}S$. Starting from a finite group $P$ with $(U_P(\mathbf 1))_{\mathbb Q}\ne0$, we will construct a pro-$p$ group $\Gamma\twoheadrightarrow P$ whose finite sections require at most $l$ generators. The same construction will describe rational cochain summands by evaluation at continuous homomorphisms from $\Gamma$ to finite groups. These evaluation coordinates will allow integration to impose the arithmetic conditions used in [sec:profinite](#sec:profinite). The construction uses finite-groupoid ambidexterity and elementary abelian sharpness, without requiring $\Gamma$ to be abelian.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### The rational tensor category and occurrence

Let $\mathcal C$ be the homotopy category of dualizable objects in $\mathrm{Sp}_{T(l)}$, with its local tensor product. Write $\mathcal C_{\mathbb Q}$ for the idempotent completion of the category with the same objects and $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal C_{\mathbb Q}}(X,Y)
 =\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathcal C}(X,Y)\otimes\mathbb Q
 \qquad (X,Y\in\mathcal C).$$ The tensor product extends to this category; its algebras are algebra objects in this ordinary tensor category. All algebra and module identities below are inherited from the local category and then rationalized. In particular, this operation is distinct from ordinary rationalization of spectra, which we denote by $(-)_{\mathbb Q}$.

<a id="eq:cochain-products"></a>
For a finite groupoid $Y$, put $A_Y=\mathbf 1^Y$, and abbreviate $A_{BF}$ to $A_F$. Finite-groupoid ambidexterity makes $A_Y$ dualizable and gives natural algebra isomorphisms $$\label{eq:cochain-products}
 A_{Y\times Z}\simeq A_Y\otimes A_Z,
 \qquad
 A_{Y\sqcup Z}\simeq A_Y\times A_Z.$$ Here and throughout this section tensors are local tensors before passing to $\mathcal C_{\mathbb Q}$. These facts follow from <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[CSY](#ref-CSY), Theorem 5.3.1 and Propositions 3.3.1, 3.3.7]. For a finite covering $f:Y\to Z$, the transfer $f_!:A_Y\to A_Z$ satisfies base change and the projection formula.

Write $\mathbb A(F)$ for the Burnside ring of a finite group $F$. Sending an $F$-set $S$ to the transfer of $1$ along $S//F\to BF$ gives a ring map $$\mathbb A(F)\longrightarrow\pi_0 A_F.$$ The rational mark isomorphism $\mathbb A(F)\otimes\mathbb Q\cong\prod_{(I\le F)}\mathbb Q$ has primitive idempotents $e_I^F$, characterized by the marks $|(e_I^F)^J|=1$ if $J$ is conjugate to $I$ and $0$ otherwise. Their images split $A_F$ into commutative algebra summands in $\mathcal C_{\mathbb Q}$. A finite group $L$ *occurs* if $$D_L:=e_L^L A_L\ne0.$$ The unit of $D_L$ is the image of $e_L^L$ in $\pi_0 A_L\otimes\mathbb Q$. Since its identity is multiplication by this class, $D_L\ne0$ precisely when this unit class is nonzero.

<a id="stmt-lem-occurrence-fixed-points"></a>
<div id="lem:occurrence-fixed-points" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:general-full-mark-comparison"></a>
**Lemma 4**. *For every finite group $L$ and ordinary commutative ring spectrum $R$, there is an equivalence of rational commutative ring spectra $$\label{eq:general-full-mark-comparison}
 e_L^L(R^{BL})_{\mathbb Q}\simeq\bigl(U_L(R)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}.$$ In particular, $$D_L\ne0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \bigl(U_L(\mathbf 1)\bigr)_{\mathbb Q}\ne0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-occurrence-fixed-points"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-occurrence-fixed-points</summary>

*Proof.* For any genuine $L$-spectrum $X$, categorical fixed points commute with ordinary rationalization: their homotopy groups are represented by suspensions of the compact genuine sphere. In the rational genuine category, $e_L^L X_{\mathbb Q}$ restricts to zero at every proper subgroup, since $e_L^L$ does. The isotropy separation sequence for the family of proper subgroups consequently identifies $$(e_L^L X_{\mathbb Q})^L\simeq
 \Phi^L(e_L^L X_{\mathbb Q})\simeq(\Phi^L X)_{\mathbb Q}.$$ Apply this to the already formed cofree spectrum $X=\mathop{\mathrm{Bor}}_L(R)$, whose categorical fixed points are $R^{BL}$. The comparison respects multiplication and identifies the full idempotent unit with the unit of $(U_L(R))_{\mathbb Q}$, proving <a href="#eq:general-full-mark-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:general-full-mark-comparison">[eq:general-full-mark-comparison]</a>. A unital ring spectrum is zero exactly when its unit is zero. Taking $R=\mathbf 1$ and using the preceding unit criterion for $D_L$ proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-occurrence-sections"></a>
<div id="prop:occurrence-sections" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5**. *Every occurring finite group is a $p$-group. The class of occurring groups is closed under subgroups and quotients. If $A$ is any section of an occurring group, its minimum number of generators satisfies $d(A)\le l$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-occurrence-sections"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-occurrence-sections</summary>

<a id="eq:induced-top-mark"></a>
*Proof.* We first record how a subgroup summand detects occurrence. If $I\le F$, the mark formula gives $$\label{eq:induced-top-mark}
 \operatorname{ind}_I^F(e_I^I)
   =|N_F(I):I|\,e_I^F.$$ Indeed a subgroup has a nonzero mark on the left only when it is conjugate to $I$, and the $I$-mark is $|N_F(I):I|$. Covering transfer carries the full $I$-unit to the right side of <a href="#eq:induced-top-mark" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:induced-top-mark">[eq:induced-top-mark]</a>. Thus $e_I^F A_F\ne0$ implies $D_I\ne0$.

Let $S$ be a Sylow $p$-subgroup of $F$. The class $[F/S]\in\pi_0 A_F$ is a unit. To see this, regard it as an endomorphism of the constant unit in $\mathop{\mathrm{Fun}}(BF,\mathrm{Sp}_{T(l)})$. Its value at the point is multiplication by $[F:S]$, a $p$-local unit, and equivalences of diagrams are detected at the point. If $F$ is not a $p$-group, the full $F$-mark of $[F/S]$ is zero. Hence $e_F^F[F/S]=0$, and invertibility forces $D_F=0$.

For a quotient $q:L\twoheadrightarrow Q$, pullback of $e_Q^Q$ has full $L$-mark $1$. Its image therefore acts as the identity on $D_L$. If the full $Q$-unit were zero, its pullback would be zero, contradicting occurrence of $L$. This proves quotient closure.

<a id="eq:subgroup-unit-map"></a>
